ZAV Architects extends house in Iran with ombre "architectural limbs"

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Photography by Parham Taghioff unless otherwise stated

A series of overlapping arches shifts in colour from terracotta to blue on this home extension on the outskirts of Tehran, designed by Iranian studio ZAV Architects.

Named Golmorad, the colourful extension transforms the residence from a weekend villa, originally designed by Iranian studio Firouz Firouz, into a permanent home in the town of Kouhsar.

ZAV Architects‘ intervention extends both ends of the home into three adjacent orchard plots acquired by the client.

Constructed from masonry, the extension is coated in plaster to blend in with the earthen exterior of the original home, while its curved geometries set it apart from its predecessor.

Aiming to appear as if organically growing from the home, Golmorad emerges as a series of overlapping arched roofs.

At one end, the vaulted extension branches into two curved forms that gradually decrease in height – one wing containing a bedroom and ensuite, and the other an open-plan kitchen, living and dining room that opens onto an outdoor pool.

Openings between the overlapping roof planes let light into the home’s interior.

“Rather than approaching the extension as either a replication of the original structure or a distinctly separate addition, the project explores a third possibility: an architecture that emerges from within the existing building itself,” ZAV Architects told Dezeen.

“This intervention can be understood as a process of grafting, in which new needs and aspirations are grafted onto the existing body of the villa, giving rise to new architectural limbs that branch out and grow from it,” it continued.

“It is as though the original building, while retaining its underlying architectural logic rooted in thick-earth construction, has developed new and distinct organs of its own.”

ZAV Architects created a gradient effect across the building with terracotta tones fading into blue, intending to strengthen the appearance of the extension growing from the home.

“The uniform colour of the original villa develops into a chromatic spectrum that radiates outward from the existing core towards the newly formed branches,” the studio said.

“This transition makes the process of transformation visible, with each segmented shell expressing a distinct stage in the building’s evolution.”

Golmorad is shortlisted in the house extension category of this year’s Dezeen Awards. It recently won the awards’ public vote alongside a playground also designed by ZAV Architects.

Last year, ZAV Architects was among the winners of the Aga Khan Award for Architecture for the Majara Residence and Community Redevelopment, a colourful domed cultural retreat on the Iranian island of Hormuz.

Photography by Parham Taghioff unless otherwise stated

Airbnb Opens Its API to Business Travel, Starting With Perk: Exclusive

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Start your subscription today and be the first to all travel industry scoops.

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Airbnb has courted business travelers for a decade, but corporate bookings mostly happened off to the side, through referral deals and workarounds. Letting Perk tap its API puts live Airbnb listings inside a corporate booking tool for what Perk says is the first time. Rival travel management companies are undoubtedly on the way.

Perk, a business travel and spend management platform, will announce Tuesday it’s the first travel management company to integrate Airbnb’s API for business travel.

Employees of Perk clients can now search Airbnb listings with live pricing and availability, book them within company travel policy, and send them for approval alongside flights, cars and rail, without leaving Perk.

Until now, Perk customers who wanted an Airbnb had to go through Perk’s concierge service, pasting a listing link and dates, and then waiting for staff to book it. Even with that manual process, Perk generated Airbnb bookings in the “double-digit millions” last year, Kristina Carter, Perk’s vice president of global partnerships, told Skift. This year it has already surpassed that total, she said.

Founded in 2015, Perk, formerly known as TravelPerk, has headquarters in London and Boston.

Perk, the business travel and spend management platform formerly known as TravelPerk, said it is the first travel management company to integrate Airbnb's API for business travel. Employees of Perk clients can now search Airbnb listings with live pricing and availability, book within company travel policy, and route bookings for manager approval alongside flights, cars, and rail. The integration follows Airbnb's exclusive B2B short-term rental deal with American Express Travel, and comes after CEO Brian Chesky told investors on the third-quarter earnings call that partnerships are one of Airbnb's biggest untapped growth opportunities. Perk's data shows Airbnb filling a different need than hotels: Airbnb nights booked through Perk rose 94% over two years, stays average nine nights, and 78% of bookings since January 2024 have been for entire homes or apartments.

Re Protocol Tops $400M in Deposits as Onchain Reinsurance Yield Attracts Capital

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Re Protocol says deposits across its reUSD and reUSDe products have exceeded $400 million, putting a relatively unfamiliar type of real-world asset exposure into the spotlight: insurance and reinsurance. Its November 4 announcement says the latest $100 million arrived in 40 days, about 80% faster than the previous comparable step.

Re Protocol says deposits across its reUSD and reUSDe products have exceeded $400 million, putting a relatively unfamiliar type of real-world asset exposure into the spotlight: insurance and reinsurance. Its November 4 announcement says the latest $100 million arrived in 40 days, about 80% faster than the previous comparable step. The growth is notable, but investors need to understand what supports the yield before treating the products as another ordinary stablecoin account.

The protocol says combined deposits into its two offerings have passed $400 million and that it has accrued more than $13 million in yield for users. These are figures supplied by the project, not an independent measure of long-run investment performance. Deposits are also different from revenue, profit or insurance premiums earned. A rapid increase in capital can reflect demand for a new product, incentives or movements between competing yield strategies. That makes the pace of growth relevant without proving that the underlying returns can be maintained as the pool becomes larger or market conditions deteriorate.

Re Protocol's approach links digital asset capital to insurance-related risk. Reinsurance companies are paid for absorbing portions of risks originally taken by insurers, so returns ultimately depend on underwriting, the frequency and severity of claims, contract structures and the reserves kept to meet obligations. That risk does not behave exactly like lending stablecoins against volatile crypto collateral. A quiet claims period can look attractive; a major insured event or unexpected loss development may expose vulnerabilities that were not visible in recent annualized performance figures. For depositors, understanding who bears losses is more important than a headline rate.

The company cites seven-day annualized yields of 6.82% for reUSD and 11.82% for reUSDe as of October 30. A seven-day observation is a short sample, not a fixed annual coupon or a promised payout. Rates can reflect product design, incentives, risk profile and the timing of income recognition. Comparing one yield to a bank savings rate or a government bond without accounting for custody, redemption, liquidity and underwriting exposure would create a misleading impression of the trade-off. Investors should examine disclosures on withdrawal mechanics, reserve treatment, any protective structures and how losses are allocated.

Putting insurance-related strategies on a blockchain can simplify accounting, transfers and access for eligible participants, but it also introduces smart contract and operational dependencies. A digital token representing an interest in a strategy is not automatically the same as a bank deposit, a fully collateralized dollar or a publicly traded security. The legal rights attached to the token matter if a strategy enters stress or if liquidity dries up. Independent contract assessments, custody arrangements and transparent reporting may improve confidence, yet none can guarantee against unexpected claims or structural mismatches.

The next meaningful disclosures would include sustained capital retention, breakdowns of yield sources, realized claims experience, audit scope, redemption performance and changes in the size of the risk pool. Those numbers would help determine whether Re Protocol is building a durable bridge between regulated insurance and decentralized markets or merely benefiting from a temporary appetite for yield. The onchain insurance thesis is worth watching, but the key question is what depositors are actually being paid to bear.

Primary source: Re Protocol company announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s reporting on institutional investors taking exposure to DeFi markets.

The little-known new face called up by England: Can you name the star brought in to replace Ezri Konsa?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published: 05:58 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 07:06 EST, 10 November 2026

Thomas Tuchel has made a first-time England call-up to replace Ezri Konsa - but casual fans could be forgiven if they do not recognise the latest Three Lions addition.

He is not a widely-recognised face among the stars from the Premier League and other top European leagues in Tuchel's selection but James Hill, 24, has starred for Bournemouth in their eight-match winning run under Marco Rose this season.

The central defender, who started his career at Fleetwood Town, has enjoyed a dream few months: He married partner Tara Rose in June, welcomed his first child in August and has now had his maiden England call.

England announced today that Arsenal defender Konsa will not be in contention to face Croatia at Wembley on Thursday night - although he will be fit to potentially face Spain away on Sunday - and Hill has been called up in his place.

Hill has played for England's Under 20s and Under 21s but this is his first senior call. He was signed by Bournemouth for just £1.2million in 2022 - beating Sheffield United, Burnley, Leeds, Southampton, Tottenham and even Barcelona, who all checked on his progress.

Bournemouth's official X account posted today: 'Congratulations, Hilly. Our defender has earned a first senior @England call-up.'

James Hill - pictured with new wife Tara - has been called up to the latest England squad (Source: Instagram/james_hill_10)

Hill has starred for Bournemouth in their eight-match winning run under Marco Rose

Hill's route to the top is a fascinating one. He attended a trial day at Fleetwood as a 14-year-old and managed to impress them enough to earn place at their new academy.

Interestingly, Hill did not play for a football team of any kind before he was 10, simply kicking a ball around with friends and playing in the garden to hone his skills.

Blackpool opted against signing him after a trial and he was then dropped by Bolton after a short stint.

It proved to be a shrewd piece of talent identification from Fleetwood, with Hill later becoming the club's record sale, eclipsing the fee they received for Jamie Vardy.

And having signed for the Cherries in 2022, Hill has now established himself as a key member of Marco Rose's side.

He's played every minute of every Premier League match this season at the heart of the defence and clearly Tuchel has been impressed.

Speaking before his call-up, Hill said: 'It’s every kid’s dream, isn’t it? You grow up watching the Three Lions on TV, for the World Cup, the Euros. It’s one of the greatest honours you can have in football.'

Football is in the blood for Hill. His father, Matt, made more than 550 appearances in the Football League for the likes of Preston North End, Wolverhampton Wanderers, Sheffield United and Queens Park Rangers as a defender.

Hill played for England at age group level, pictured here kissing his now wife after a game

Hill's younger brother, Tyler, is also in the game, playing for Torquay United in National League South.

But Matt did not pressure his sons into the sport, with James previously telling the Times: 'He didn’t want me to feel as though it’s a job.

'He wanted me to feel like I’m wanting to be here because I love doing it. This is my passion. It brings me happiness. He wanted me to come to that conclusion, not for him to make that decision for me.'

Hill was a well-rounded sportsman and his secondary school did not even play football. Instead, he excelled as a fly-half for the rugby side and impressed as a javelin thrower.

He attributes his success as being aided by trying his hand at different disciplines and it is paying off in abundance having had to largely bide his time before earning a first-team spot under Andoni Iraola last term, with loans to Blackburn and Hearts helping him develop.

Hill's progress flew somewhat under the radar somewhat for many fans outside of Bournemouth and his career peak of an England call-up coincides with domestic bliss.

Having married Tara in the summer, they welcomed their daughter Edie two months later.

Hill, pictured, heading the ball away against Ipswich has become one of the best defenders in the Premier League

'Becoming a dad is one of the most special things I’ve ever experienced in my life,' he said. 'There isn’t a manual though, it’s just like, "Congratulations, you’ve got a baby". Then the door shuts! Hang on a minute. What am I supposed to do next? Is there a book for this? My wife has been absolutely incredible.'

He regularly shares pictures from both his professional and personal life on social media, putting wedding snaps alongside action shots of Bournemouth games.

Hill's England call-up will no doubt introduce him to a whole new audience previously unaware of the defender's capabilities, with only 34,000 Instagram followers at present.

The 24-year-old must now impress Tuchel enough to earn some game time when England play Croatia on Thursday in their next Nations League encounter.

Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg breaks silence on Crystal Palace links

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Kim Hellberg is continuing to earn admirers as Middlesbrough's pursuit of promotion to the Premier League toils on, and with new interest emerging from Crystal Palace ahead of Pierre Sage's increasingly-inevitable sacking, the Swede has now made his thoughts clear on a potential switch to Selhurst Park.

Middlesbrough will fancy their chances of successfully avenging last season's heartbreaking last-grasp play-off final defeat to Hull City after enjoying a strong start to the opening months of the 2026/27 Championship campaign, which has seen Hellberg's side lock down second-place heading into the November international break with eight victories, five draws and just two defeats from 15 fixtures to date.

Level on points with both league leaders Birmingham City and third-placed Stoke, Middlesbrough have often appeared the team to beat in the second-tier thus far and will intend on picking up where they left off once they return to action in two weeks' time with a double-header against Bristol City and a fellow promotion rival in Swansea - having endured a slight dip in form during Will Lankshear's injury-enforced absence.

The Teessiders have shown that, under Hellberg's tuition, they are capable of finally ending their top-flight exile and getting over the finish line once and for all, which is exactly why the Riverside Stadium faithful will want nothing more than recent links to Crystal Palace to go away.

Interestingly, though, Hellberg himself has now broken his silence on the speculation in a crucial new development.

Hellberg's exploits on Teesside appear to be gaining wider attention, and a report from Sky Sports on Sunday evening named the hugely-popular 38-year-old as one of seven candidates on Crystal Palace's managerial radar alongside the likes of Sean Dyche, Frank Lampard, Vitor Matos of Swansea and leading contender Thomas Frank.

This update, of course, comes amid the impending sacking of Pierre Sage, which is yet to be formally finalised but seemingly represents an inevitability after the Frenchman left the Eagles rooted to the foot of the top-flight table with just two wins from his opening ten encounters heading into the latest two-week window.

Hellberg has publicly spoken of his dream to manage in the Premier League, but that's precisely where Middlesbrough could be headed on his watch and the Swedish boss has now reacted to links with the likely-soon-to-be-vacancy at Selhurst Park.

Having only been appointed at Boro just under twelve months ago, Hellberg discussed the reported interest with Swedish outlet Fotbollskanalen and reiterated his desire to focus on the job at hand with the Championship promotion hopefuls.

Birmingham City and Middlesbrough are both plotting shock moves for Leeds United-linked Lorient star Arthur Avom, Football League World understands

When asked how he feels about speculation linking his services with Palace, he explained: "Absolutely nothing, absolutely nothing.

"It's always nice to be on lists like that, if that's the case - it shows you've done something right. But I think it's mostly just talk, and for me it's all about doing a good job here.

"We actually have a chance to get promoted this year. We feel that there is a chance, but then it's clear that it will be difficult.

"We are one of five, six teams, maybe not the biggest favourites, but behind them I think we have a good chance.

"My focus is here and hopefully we can get up, that would have been fantastic of course."

It's abundantly clear that the entirety of Hellberg's focus is on the task at hand, which is getting Boro back to the promised land at the second time of asking - and they've got a real strong shot of doing just that on his watch.

For a head coach of Hellberg's quality - and we scarcely see that in the Championship, truth be told - it could be easy to be swayed by the opportunity to manage at the highest level.

However, Middlesbrough's own project is attractive, with Steve Gibson rewarding Hellberg with no shortage of financial backing to bolster Boro's squad, and they've only strengthened after sanctioning the big-money departure of Hayden Hackney to Everton over the summer.

Indeed, Boro have one of the Championship's strongest and deepest sides, and from defence to attack, stars such as Adilson Malanda, Aidan Morris and most notably the aforementioned Lankshear are all long-term assets with serious future resale value.

Hellberg has a gifted and high-ceiling squad at his disposal, and missing out on promotion with what he has would undoubtedly represent a failure.

That said, there's no better coach to finally return Boro to the big time and, having only appointed Hellberg after Rob Edwards' contentious and ultimately ill-fated decision to jump ship for Wolves merely months after arriving in the North East, they'll only be pleased and relieved in equal measure to see much more loyalty, dedication and focus from the sought-after Swede.

Make no mistake about it, Middlesbrough won't find a finer opportunity to end their promotion hoodoo as Hellberg continues to kick on with one of the strongest Boro squads we've seen in recent history.

Albeit expensively-assembled, there's no denying the vast quality across Boro's squad. Radek Vitek between the sticks and the likes of Callum Brittain, Neto Borges, Malanda and Ash Phillips provides a second-tier backline stiffer than most, even if Hellberg's emphasis on committing bodies forward and dominating possession in attacking thirds can see the Teessiders occasionally exploited in quick counter-transitions.

You won't find many stronger and more multi-functional midfielders at this level than Middlesbrough's, though, which features Sebastian Berhalter, Riley McGree and Morris - and even versatile French defender Malanda.

The attacking line is just as impressive, and when Hellberg can call upon the joint double-act brilliance of Amario Cozier-Duberry out wide and top-scorer Lankshear alongside Tommy Conway and Jeremy Sarmiento, it's no surprise that only two sides are averaging more goals a game.

Middlesbrough fans, how relieved are you by Kim Hellberg's reaction to Crystal Palace? Are you worried at all that the Swede could leave before the job is done? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

Middlesbrough have serious, well-stocked quality in almost every area of the pitch and are a joy to watch at full throttle, and Hellberg himself only knows that all-too-well and won't be willing to part with this talented and exciting squad easily.

Boro, in truth, really should have held their nerve to seal a top-two finish last term, but the opportunity for promotion is even greater now with an improved squad and a route back to the top-flight by the time May rolls around may be exactly what's required to ensure Hellberg's long-term future remains at the Riverside.

Siemens to buy chip debugging specialist Lauterbach to grow its EDA unit

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The German firm makes TRACE32 tools that help engineers find software bugs in chips before they are built.

Lauterbach’s TRACE32 debug and trace tools

Siemens has agreed to buy Lauterbach, a German company that makes tools for finding and fixing bugs in chips and the software that runs on them, but the price was not disclosed.

Founded in 1979 by brothers Lothar and Stephan Lauterbach, the company is based in Höhenkirchen, near Munich. It employs about 170 people and made around €60m in revenue last year.

Lauterbach is best known for TRACE32, a range of debugging and tracing tools. Engineers use them to see what is happening inside a chip as its software runs, and to track down errors. The company has more than 1,200 customers, mostly in the car, chip and telecoms industries.

The deal will become part of Siemens’ electronic design automation (EDA) business, which sells software for designing and testing chips. Siemens built that business mainly through its purchase of Mentor Graphics in 2017.

Siemens already sells hardware systems that imitate a chip before it is made, so that designers can test it early. By adding Lauterbach’s tools, it wants to let customers check the chip and its software together. As a result, they could start writing and testing software before the physical chip exists, and find problems sooner.

This matters more as chips and software become harder to separate. Cars, for example, are increasingly controlled by software, while AI and connected devices need chips and code designed side by side.

Cedrik Neike, who heads Siemens’ Digital Industries division, said semiconductors are a core part of the company’s technology strategy. The deal, he said, would let customers test and improve their products before the chip is made.

“With Lauterbach, we are further extending our industry-leading EDA portfolio for semiconductor design by bringing together our premier hardware-assisted verification portfolio with its industry-leading software debugging technologies,” said Cedrik Neike, Member of the Managing Board of Siemens AG and President of Digital Industries.

For its part, Lauterbach said its customer focus would stay the same.

“Our commitment to keeping our customers at the center of everything we do remains unchanged,” said Stephan Lauterbach, the company’s co-founder and chief technology officer.

He and Thomas Ullmann will stay on at Siemens once the deal is completed. Siemens also expects the deal to bring significant extra sales.

The acquisition is expected to close in the first half of 2027, subject to regulatory approval. Besides Germany, Lauterbach has offices in the UK, Italy, France, Tunisia, the US, Japan and China.

The deal adds to Siemens’ growing push into chips and industrial AI. Elsewhere, the company has been testing humanoid robots with Nvidia at its own factory in Erlangen.

It’s also a record for Gasperini: his most prolific team on matchday 11 was Atalanta in 2019-20 with 30 goals.

Roma are now averaging 2.82 goals per game. Their driving force is Donyell Malen, who has scored eight times.

Roma, when they get past their opponents this season, want to win big. In Serie A, they’ve scored at least three goals against Udinese (5), Fiorentina (4), Lecce (4), Cagliari (3), Napoli (3, drawing), and Sassuolo (3).

All the other European big clubs are behind the Giallorossi: from PSG (2.2 goals per game) to Real Madrid (2.58). Roma’s performance is extraordinary, with two key factors standing out: Malen’s goals (8) and the ability of almost every player to beat opposing goalkeepers (12 different ones in the league).

Bitcoin CPI Reaction: 10 of 13 Cool Inflation Reports Triggered an Hourly Gain – Can Inflation Data Reverse the Downtrend?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin CPI reaction: October CPI lands at 8:30 a.m. ET, core seen at 0.22%. Cool prints lifted BTC in the first hour 10 times out of 13.

The U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics publishes October’s consumer price index (CPI) on Tuesday, November 10, at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. The Bitcoin CPI reaction that follows could set the tone for the week. The CPI is the main U.S. measure of inflation, and it will shape the Federal Reserve’s next rate decision.

Economists expect core prices, which leave out food and energy, to have risen about 0.22% in the month. That would be a slowdown from September’s 0.33%.

Economists expect growth in the core CPI to have moderated in October to around 0.22%, though the full range of estimates is broad enough to round down to 0.1% or round up to 0.3%.

Due to the government shutdown in Oct 2025, there's no tidy year-earlier figure. pic.twitter.com/CFM7ikw8fO

— Nick Timiraos (@NickTimiraos) November 9, 2026

Bitcoin trades near $74,400, still close to Sunday’s two-month low of $73,510.

CPI days move crypto. We checked Bitcoin’s first hour after the last 24 releases. When core inflation came in at 0.2% or lower, BTC rose in that hour 10 times out of 13. When it came in at 0.3% or higher, it rose only 4 times out of 10.

So which way will today’s number push Bitcoin?

The forecasts point to a calmer month. Seventeen banks and research firms surveyed by The Wall Street Journal see a median rise of 0.25% for headline CPI and 0.22% for core. Journal reporter Nick Timiraos posted the table on X.

That spread matters. The government reports CPI rounded to one decimal place. With forecasts this spread out, the core reading could print anywhere from 0.1% to 0.3%. A 0.3% would match September’s pace and read as inflation that is not cooling.

The yearly comparison will also be messy. The government shutdown in October 2025 meant no CPI was published for that month, so there is no clean year-over-year figure.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

This is the last CPI report the Federal Reserve will see before its December 8-9 meeting. Traders already expect a rate hike: on Tuesday morning, Polymarket gives an 86.5% chance of a 0.25-point increase and 11.5% for no change.

Higher rates tend to weigh on Bitcoin. They make cash and bonds pay more, which pulls money away from assets that pay no interest, like crypto.

A cool reading could shave those odds and give Bitcoin room to bounce. A hot one would lock in the hike.

DISCOVER: Fed Meeting Today: Traders Price a 95% Hold and a 79% December Hike. What Does It Mean for Crypto?

We measured the Bitcoin CPI reaction on Binance. We took the 24 CPI reports published since October 2024 and measured Bitcoin’s move in the hour after each 8:30 a.m. release.

The pattern is a lean, not a rule. The biggest drop came on February 12, 2025, when core rose 0.4% and BTC lost 1.9% in an hour. Last month’s 0.3% reading cost it 1.4%.

So far it has. BTC trades at $74,398 on Binance, about 1.2% above Sunday’s low.

On the daily chart, Bitcoin sits below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that weight recent prices more. The 200-day EMA is at $76,481 and the 50-day EMA at $79,128.

For anyone tracking the Bitcoin CPI reaction, the first hour after 8:30 a.m. is the one to watch. In 20 of the last 24 reports, Bitcoin ended the next 24 hours moving in the same direction as in that first hour.

World of Warcraft: Forever might live officially on PC, but people are playing it on phones and handhelds already

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If you're a World of Warcraft: Forever player you're almost certainly having your free time drained as you chain yourself to your desktop PC to play the game, or maybe a laptop. But for a small selection of true enthusiasts, the game is already working on mobile and handheld devices.

One such person showing off WoW: Forever on the go is none other than director of publishing at Larian, Michael Douse, who took to Twitter to show off the MMO being played on an AYN Thor device. For those who don't know, this is a fancy android handheld console, a sort of beefed up DS for the modern handheld aficionado.

"Arguably too many layers of abstraction," writes Douse. In the picture he posted, you can see the player character viewable on the Thor's top screen, while the map, text chat, skill UI, and buffs viewable on the bottom. It looks pretty cool, and is made possible due to JReese702's Github mod you can download at your leisure.

Arguably too many layers of abstraction pic.twitter.com/VjW0IX00Gg

But it's not the only mo-bell at the ball. Another Github WoW: Forever mod, WolkaraForever, gets the game playable on the iPhone Duo. As highlighted by CEO of esports company 100 Thieves Alex Wechsler on Twitter, the game looks to be ported pretty well to the phone, with mobile touchscreen controls viewable on both sides of the screen. Be warned though, as confirmed by Wechsler in a Twitter reply, it'll get your phone running hot.

As a father of 2 kids and always having wanted to play WOW, I’ve finally found a way 🥹 pic.twitter.com/eIVn0gn1WC

This coming alongside World of Warcraft: Forever's built-in (and very good) controller support could mean the game has real potential to spread far and wide across the vast array of different hardware out there. Blizzard has already hinted at plans of porting the game to other platforms, so who knows? Maybe we can expect an official mobile port to tread the path paved by these community-made mods.

GTA VI takes over miami with six real-life cars built by west coast customs

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

from a pink-and-purple sports coupe to a police-liveried supercar, the fully drivable lineup turns fictional automotive excess into something with actual horsepower.

Rockstar Games has brought six vehicles from Grand Theft Auto VI out of the screen and onto the streets of Miami, enlisting Los Angeles-based West Coast Customs to transform fictional designs into fully drivable machines. The project arrives ahead of the game’s November 19 release, translating some of its most recognizable automotive creations into physical vehicles. From classic American lowriders to contemporary supercars, the collection recreates the exaggerated personalities that have long defined the franchise’s approach to car culture.

The six vehicles retain their fictional brand names, distinctive silhouettes and elaborate finishes, with modifications extending well beyond their exterior appearance. Each has been presented alongside its digital counterpart, revealing how closely the real builds follow their virtual references. Photographs also place the collection at recognizable Miami landmarks, including Herzog & de Meuron’s 1111 Lincoln Road parking structure and the Miami Marine Stadium. The locations connect the actual city with Vice City, Rockstar’s fictional interpretation of Florida’s coastal metropolis.

Among the six creations developed by Rockstar Games in collaboration with West Coast Customs, the Grotti Cheetah draws on the proportions of classic Italian supercars, with a low-slung body, sculpted side intakes and a naturally aspirated 4.9-liter V8 engine. The customization workshop finishes the vehicle with a gradient paint treatment, lowered suspension and custom leather upholstery. The Invetero Coquette takes a more contemporary direction, pairing a 6.2-liter V8 with a striking pink-and-purple camouflage exterior. Meanwhile, the Progen Emerus embraces track-inspired engineering, combining a twin-turbocharged 4.0-liter V8 with aerodynamic bodywork, an oversized rear wing and a bright orange interior.

American automotive culture provides the references for the Albany Buccaneer and Albany Manana. The former reinterprets a 1960s lowrider with candy-green metallic paint, gold wheels, air suspension and zebra-patterned interior details. The Manana follows a different tradition, adopting the dramatically elevated stance of a donk-style custom car, complete with oversized wheels, blue bodywork and gold accents. Completing the lineup is the Pegassi Tempesta, a V10-powered supercar dressed in the fictional Vice Beach Police Department’s black-and-white livery. Apparently, even law enforcement gets a supercar in Leonida.

The project reflects the close relationship between Grand Theft Auto’s visual identity and real-world automotive customization. Throughout the series, Rockstar Games has developed fictional manufacturers and vehicles that borrow recognizable proportions and styling cues from established brands while exaggerating their defining characteristics. Translating these designs into functional machines requires more than reproducing their shapes. Paint finishes, wheel proportions, suspension setups and interior materials become essential to preserving the identity of each vehicle beyond the screen.

The resulting collection also highlights the influence of Miami’s automotive culture on the fictional world of Vice City, from extravagant lowriders to brightly colored performance cars. By placing the vehicles in the same urban environment that inspires the game, Rockstar Games brings its digital references back to their physical origins. With Grand Theft Auto VI scheduled to launch on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S on November 19, the six builds offer an unusually tangible preview of its universe, where even the most improbable designs appear perfectly at home on the road.

Exodus shares rise 10% ahead of Q3 earnings showing 7% revenue growth, $8.3 million loss

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The wallet provider posted an $8.3 million net loss, swinging from $17 million in net income in Q3 2025.

Exodus shares rose 10.07% on Monday to close at $4.48, ahead of the company's third-quarter earnings release, which showed higher revenue but a return to quarterly losses.

The NYSE American-listed company's stock remained down 8.57% over five trading days and 70.21% since the start of 2026, according to The Block's EXOD price page.

Exodus reported $32.5 million in revenue for the three months ended Sept. 30, up 7% from $30.3 million a year earlier, the company said in a statement.

Net income attributable to the company swung to an $8.3 million loss from $17 million in profit in the third quarter of 2025. Adjusted EBITDA also turned negative, with a $3.9 million loss compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $5.9 million a year earlier.

"Q3 was about execution: integrating the organization, restoring some payment capabilities, sharpening our product roadmap, and building a more disciplined operating model," the company wrote on X.

Exodus reported $1.1 billion in gross transaction volume during the quarter, alongside $1.1 billion in total swap volume. It also reported 1.6 million active cards and 1.4 million monthly active users, according to the statement.

Quarterly funded users increased 7.7% from the previous quarter to 1.4 million, while swap volume declined 3.4% from Q2.

The company has been expanding beyond its self-custodial wallet business. In June, Exodus launched Exodus Markets with Ondo Finance, offering eligible customers access to more than 200 tokenized stocks, ETFs, and other assets through its wallet on Solana.

"We are building toward a financial system where anyone can receive money, invest it, and spend it as easily as they send a message, while retaining control of their assets," Exodus CEO JP Richardson said in the statement.

Meanwhile, the company said it remains on track to reduce combined annual costs by approximately $30 million going into 2027, up from an earlier $10 million to $13 million target as it integrates Monavate and Baanx.

10-Minute Full-Body Stretch for Dog Owners

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Longtime yoga teacher Amber Burke, graduate of two YogaWorks teacher trainings and former leader of a teacher training program at the University of New Mexico-Taos, shares her favorite stretches to do before walking her dogs. You can also explore other ways to stretch your entire body.

Confession time. I love my dogs, but I don’t always love walking them. Dog walking, I have found, bears little resemblance to normal walking. It is far more physically demanding than I ever imagined in my pre-dog-owning years, and sometimes it’s even a little scary. I have wrenched my back when my two big rescue mutts decided to dart in front of me while playing tug-of-war. I have been pulled after squirrels and tugged straight into lampposts. I have fallen into bushes. I have faceplanted. It is a small miracle that my shoulders are still attached to my body and I have (almost) all of my teeth.

Even when a dog walk goes smoothly, I usually end up feeling a bit crooked, hunched over, and frazzled by the end. If, like me, you walk big dogs, two or more dogs, high-strung or strong-willed dogs, and live anywhere with squirrels, you might value some yoga poses and movements that strengthen and stretch your shoulders, improve your balance, and stabilize your back, hips, and legs to help you survive the demands of your next dog walk.

This standing practice can be done in just a few minutes before your next dog walk, or simply do one or a few of these exercises while your dogs are busy running in the dog park. No mat or lowering yourself to the ground required. You will need a strap—or dog leash, after you’ve untangled yourself from it.

The top priority for dog walkers is shoulder stability. Dogs yank, and when your dogs go after a squirrel, you don’t want your shoulders to go with them. Train your shoulders to resist every yank with this variation of Mountain Pose (Tadasana).

How to: Stand tall with your feet hip-distance apart in Mountain Pose and your arms alongside your body, squeezing inward in at your sides. Bend your elbows. Roll your shoulders up and back, and then down your back. Draw your shoulder blades toward each other, imagining that you’re creating a crease between them. Pause here for several breaths, then release.

This exercise helps strengthen the muscles that keep your shoulder blades in place and reverses that forward-rounding back hunch that might result from holding a leash in front of you.

How to: Take a hold of a leash (or strap) behind your back with your palms facing forward, about shoulder-distance apart, or if your hands meet behind you, interlace your fingers behind you. Keeping your shoulders back and your arms straight, pulse your arms up and down several times, lifting them as high as is comfortable. Repeat for several rounds, then release.

Dog walking takes your arms—and your attention—forward and down as you keep an eye on your dog, any potential obstacles, and tempting trash items. This exercise moves your body in the opposite direction, broadening across your chest and stretching the fronts of your shoulders.

How to: Reach your arms straight in front of your chest. Hold a leash between your hands, taking them slightly wider apart than shoulder-width. As you inhale, lift the leash overhead and lower it slightly behind your shoulders; as you exhale, bring it back in front of you, keeping your arms relatively straight the entire time. (If your arms bend a lot, move your hands wider on the leash.) Do this for several rounds.

You can also pause with the leash behind you, if that gives you a good stretch, for a few breaths. Then lower your arms and release the leash.

If you have more than one dog, it’s a good idea to be ready for that cumbersome moment when they criss-cross their leashes, going in opposite directions. This stretch allows you to practice that arm position by stretching the muscles along the backs of your shoulders and in between your shoulder blades.

How to: Cross your arms at the elbows in front of your chest. Your arms will be slightly bent. Make soft fists, then reach your hands away from each other as if you are trying to straighten your arms in opposite directions. Pause here for a few breaths, then release your arms and switch sides so the opposite arm is on top.

It‘s all too easy to twist an ankle if your dog pulls you sideways while you’re stepping off a curb or if you’re simply paying more attention to your furry friend than your own footing. Heel lifts help to strengthen your ankles and your balance.

How to: Stand with your feet together or hip-distance apart and your arms by your sides. Gaze at a steady point on the ground or, for more of a balance challenge, at a fixed point on the horizon. Press down with the balls of your feet. As you inhale, lift your heels; as you exhale, slowly lower your heels, without letting the ankles splay to the sides. Do this several times.

When your dog jumps on you unexpectedly, the last thing you want to do is take a tumble. This dynamic pose variation helps you cultivate leg strength and balance to prepare you to stand strong.

How to: With your feet together or hip-distance apart, gaze at an unmoving point in front of you. Press down into the balls of your feet, then lift your heels. Bend your knees, lean your chest slightly forward, sink your hips toward the ground, and reach your arms straight out in front of your chest in a Chair Pose variation. For more of a challenge, bend your knees more deeply to bring your hips closer to the ground, and try to keep your posture more upright instead of leaning forward, drawing your belly toward your spine. Pause for several breaths, then lower your heels and arms as you return to standing.

If your dogs have ever tried to take off, you know how important it is to be able lower your center of gravity fast so you can resist their pull. Get ready to drop down into a sturdy position by practicing Squat (Malasana).

How to: Stand with your feet a little wider than hip-width and turn your toes out slightly. Bend your knees and lower your hips as far as you like, tracking your knees over your toes. Bring your elbows inside your knees and your hands together in prayer position in Squat. Broaden across your chest and lift your heart space. Stay here for a few breaths, then press your feet into the ground to rise back to standing.

Dog walkers do a lot of forward folding—we bend down to pet our dogs, to pick up after them, to take their harnesses on and off, and to pry things that they really shouldn’t be eating out of their mouths. Pyramid Pose (Parsvottanasana) warms up your hamstrings and mobilizes your lower back to help you do all that with greater ease.

How to: Press down through your left foot and step your right foot around 12 inches behind you. Place your hands on your hips. Hinge from your hips and fold your chest forward as much as is comfortable, or about parallel to the ground. Bend your front knee if you like. Stay in Pyramid Pose, petting a dog if you like, or drape your upper body over your front leg and let your head relax for more of a more intense stretch. To come out of the pose, walk your hands up your front leg. Then change sides.

Dog walkers need to be able to preserve their stability, and also change speeds and directions on the fly. You might need to dart sideways out of the way of another walker who isn’t paying attention, or quickly escape an approaching off-leash dog. Use this move to practice fast footwork and energize your legs.

How to: Standing with your feet together, hinge from your hips and reach your chest forward. Cross your right foot behind your left, planting the ball of your right foot as far outside your left foot as feels comfortable. Bend your left leg slightly. Reach your arms toward the left, then step your right foot back to the starting position. Then cross your left foot behind and outside your right foot and reach your arms toward the right. Move from side to side several times, building momentum and going as slowly or quickly as you want. Then return to standing.

The front of your thighs might be feeling the work of the previous poses—and months or years of dog walking. Dancer’s Pose (Natarajasana) provides a stretch for the quadriceps, as well as a backbend that gently counterstretches all the forward reaching and bending of dog walking. It also gives you another chance to strengthen your balance.

How to: Shift your weight into your left foot. Bend your right knee and draw your right heel toward your buttock. Loop your leash or strap around your right foot or ankle, draw the strap over your right shoulder, and hold it with one or both hands in Dancer Pose variation. Stay here for a few breaths, or use both hands to lift the strap overhead and press your right shin behind you, arching into a backbend. Draw your elbows toward each other and lift your chest. Lower down and change sides.

Do you find yourself holding onto the leash for dear life? All that gripping can create tightness in the hands and wrists. Here’s a way to relieve that tension.

How to: Reach your arms in front of your chest and interlace your fingers with your palms facing forward. Straighten your arms as you press your palms away from you. Stay here for several breaths, or lift your arms overhead to add a full-body stretch. Then release your hands and lower your arms by your sides.

In addition to stretching your back and hamstrings, Standing Forward Bend (Uttanasana) can also be mentally relaxing, allowing you to breathe out the stresses that come with daily life…and dog walking.

How to: Plant your feet hip-distance apart. Fold forward, walking your hands down your legs and bending your knees as much as you need. Place your hands on the ground or your shins in Standing Forward Bend, grasp opposite elbows, or let your arms hang toward the ground in Rag Doll. Relax your neck. Pause here for several breaths, then walk your hands up your legs to return to standing.

After you’ve returned home from your dog walk, give yourself several minutes of well-deserved rest. You made it!

How to: Recline on your yoga mat, with or without dogs. (Only you know if they will enhance your relaxation or distract you from it!) Sigh out any remaining tension. Allow your shoulders to soften toward the floor with your  hands out to the sides or resting on your body in  Savasana. You don’t need to be on guard anymore. Give yourself over to relaxation, the way dogs know how to do when the fun is over. For a few minutes, surrender your weight to the earth, and let yourself be a big warm animal, gently breathing.

Under pressure, Big Walk studio reveals it used generative AI "sparingly" while making its hit 2026 game

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"It has become a matter of public interest."

Australian developer House House has revealed it used generative AI "sparingly" to help make celebrated, indie, multiplayer puzzle game Big Walk.

In a statement sent to Aftermath - following a judging fallout related to the Independent Games Festival - House House revealed it had used large language models primarily to help debug the game's code.

"In the final stretch of Big Walk's development, LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs," the developer said. "In some cases, snippets from LLM responses were copied as a starting point to be modified into game code - we'd estimate that well under 0.1 percent of Big Walk's code had anything to do with this usage, and generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game."

House House added: "The primary use case for House House's use of LLMs was to research, via chatbots, issues we encountered with third-party code."

The studio's revelation comes after a sizeable number of IGF jurors - 19 of them - issued a statement saying they would not vote for any game that used generative AI during its development. This was done after seeing "a significant increase" in entry submissions involving generative AI use, which "set off some fairly intense discussion and disagreement across the judge and jury pools". And rather than not vote for these in silence, a collection of jurors decided to take a public stand.

House House hadn't disclosed Big Walk generative AI use anywhere before disclosing it for its IGF submission. "We have not made any disclosure on Steam as the platform does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage," the studio explained. "We are posting this disclosure as it has become a matter of public interest."

House House added: "This is not an endorsement of, or argument for, the use of AI tools, however minimal. We have some concern that voluntary disclosures like this might contribute to the normalisation of this technology, or its treatment as 'inevitable'. We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to video games and our world, and apologise that Big Walk's LLM usage may not meet their standards.

"Big Walk was developed without an internal AI usage policy in place, and we consider that lack of policy a failure. We will work to have a more comprehensive policy going forwards."

It's a revelation that's likely to leave a taint of some degree on one of this year's brightest stars - a game Eurogamer awarded a full five stars to in its Big Walk review.

Anthropic Seeks Record $88.5B Tied To Public Market Debut

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Anthropic is seeking to secure up to $88.5 billion in its planned IPO and a concurrent private placement, potentially making it the largest-ever fundraise tied to a public market debut.

On Tuesday, the AI giant submitted an updated S-1 filing for its upcoming public offering, unveiling new details including a proposed share price range offering size. The offering could set a fully diluted valuation of up to $1.4 trillion for the San Francisco company.

Anthropic’s expected valuation comes in below earlier projections, with many investors reportedly forecasting something closer to $2 trillion. That said, even at $1.4 trillion, Anthropic would still be going public at the second-highest valuation in history, topped only by SpaceX’s June debut.

The offering should also provide Anthropic with plentiful capital to pursue its expensive expansion and infrastructure investment plans.

For the IPO portion of its fundraise, Anthropic plans to sell just over 800 million shares 1 at $70 to $78 each. At the high end, that would bring in around $62.5 billion — the largest ever IPO fundraise after SpaceX’s $75 billion offering.

Anthropic also expects to pull in another $26 billion from a private placement slated to close immediately following the IPO. Investors in this include AMD, Broadcom, Google, Micron Technology, Microsoft, Nvidia and TSMC.

Put together, it’s a record-setting capital haul for a company making its market debut, which Anthropic is expected to do later this month on Nasdaq under the ticker symbol ANT.

Anthropic also recently updated its financial disclosures, showing results through September of this year.

For the first nine months of the year, the company had $33.2 billion in revenue, up from $2.5 billion in the same period a year ago. It’s an astounding growth rate, albeit Anthropic is already known for this.

The company claims it has the fastest enterprise adoption of any technology in history, growing revenue from zero at commercial launch in the first quarter of 2023 to $17 billion for the three months ended Sept. 30, 2026.

As of yet, however, it’s not profitable. Anthropic posted an operating loss of $1.3 billion for the first nine months of the year, down from $5.2 billion a year earlier. Its net loss widened to $104 billion, up from $25.1 billion a year ago.

The total offering is for 825 million shares, of which 23.6 million will be from selling shareholders.↩

XRP Ledger Introduces Quantum-Proof Security Upgrade to Users

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

As the XRP Ledger developers continue to make committed efforts to make the network fully quantum-resistant in the coming year, the team has just proposed a major upgrade that has sparked a buzz across the XRP community.

Sparking fresh excitement across the XRP community earlier today, the XRP Ledger (XRPL) Foundation has disclosed plans to introduce quantum-resistant accounts to users.

The team revealed plans to introduce a new feature to the XRP Ledger to offer users strict protection against future quantum computing threats without paying additional transaction fees.

While it is yet to be implemented, the proposed design will allow users to add a NIST-approved ML-DSA-44 signing key to their accounts and disable their existing keys.

This way, it will become harder for potential quantum attacks to compromise accounts, especially those whose public keys have already been exposed.

Apart from the extra layer of protection the upgrade promises to offer users, its cost-effective design has stirred more interest among the XRP community.

Notably, users will not have to pay higher transaction fees to access the additional security, as the proposed feature allows XRP holders to strengthen their account protection without worrying about extra transaction costs.

The proposed upgrade sends relief across the crypto ecosystem as it addresses concerns about the possibility of increasingly powerful quantum computers eventually breaking some of the cryptographic systems currently used to secure blockchains and cryptocurrencies.

While the upgrade is yet to launch, the team revealed that it has scheduled a migration study for the first half of 2027.

The study is aimed at examining how existing accounts can transition to the new security system and what the process would involve for users.

The proposed security upgrade has further sparked more optimism as it is also part of Ripple's broader aim of achieving full quantum protection by 2028.

Schwab adds Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink as crypto trading slows

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Schwab adds three crypto assets as trading activity weakens across major platforms and Wall Street seeks retail demand.

Charles Schwab added Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink to its direct crypto trading service on Nov. 9, giving customers five assets to trade just as the industry's latest disclosures show fewer active users on some platforms and smaller trades on others.

Coinbase's monthly transacting users averaged 7.4 million in the third quarter, down from 9.3 million a year earlier.

Banks and brokerages could win existing holders' business without expanding overall participation, while easier access can redistribute trading revenue even when the overall market is subdued.

Schwab's token additions follow early access in May and a rollout to all eligible retail clients in October. Its service now offers Bitcoin, Ethereum and the three newly added tokens.

Fidelity's current trading lineup includes Bitcoin, Ethereum, Fidelity Digital Dollar, Litecoin and Solana through separate Fidelity Crypto accounts. SoFi began a phased consumer rollout on Nov. 11, 2025, bringing crypto into the same app as its other financial services.

These offerings place crypto inside financial relationships customers may already have.

Coinbase disclosed its user decline in its Oct. 29 filing, attributing its year-over-year decline in monthly transacting users primarily to fewer trading users under current market conditions.

Quarterly-average MTUs: 9.3 million in Q3 2025 and 7.4 million in Q3 2026. Includes passive activity and some non-revenue transfers; this is not a count of unique investors leaving crypto.

The MTU measure includes passive activity and some non-revenue-generating transfers, and the quarterly figure averages monthly measurements.

Coinbase also warns that MTUs can overstate unique consumers. The decline measures activity on Coinbase rather than departures from crypto as a whole.

Gemini's second-quarter results put exchange trading volume at $4 billion, down from $11 billion a year earlier, while monthly transacting users increased 11% to 580,000.

More users were present, but substantially less money changed hands.

At eToro, preliminary August figures showed the invested amount per crypto trade fell to $97 from $325 a year earlier, a 70% decline. The number of crypto trades fell a much smaller 4%, to 5.3 million.

The platform's funded accounts rose 18% to 4.36 million, including 110,000 accounts from acquisitions.

Robinhood also reported third-quarter app crypto notional volume of $21 billion, down 48% year over year, and crypto transaction revenue of $120 million, down 55%.

Outside individual platforms, Gallup's June ownership survey estimated that 9% of US adults held crypto, down from 14% in 2025. Its weighted probability-based sample covered 2,043 adults.

The disclosures show softness across several businesses and in one national ownership measure, but users can hold multiple accounts, ownership differs from trading, and the reporting periods vary.

CoinGecko's third-quarter report put trading volume on the top 10 centralized spot exchanges at $1.65 trillion, down 14.2% from $1.93 trillion in the second quarter.

CoinGecko's broader average daily trading-volume measure recovered from $58 billion in July to $95 billion in September. Outstanding positions on the top 10 perpetual-futures exchanges also increased 40.6%, ending the quarter above $100 billion.

Coinbase Institutional's Nov. 2 analysis then reported that October spot volume fell 16%, while options volume rose 9%.

CoinGecko's eight-exchange liquidity report, updated Sept. 30, found Bitcoin's sampled order-book liquidity nearly 50% above its 2025 comparison, while Solana's fell more than 28.5%.

Trading activity and available market depth are moving in different directions.

In Schwab's survey released Oct. 7, six in 10 current crypto investors expected to increase their holdings over the following 12 months.

A poll by the National Cryptocurrency Association and Harris offers another indication of potential demand. In an online survey of 2,017 US adults conducted Sept. 15-16, 42% said crypto access would make them more likely to open a bank account or use it for more of their financial needs.

Even the size of the potential audience is uncertain, since Schwab reported 21% crypto ownership in its unweighted online sample of Americans ages 21-75, compared with Gallup's 9% estimate from a weighted probability-based survey of adults 18 and older.

Crypto.com's on-chain-based estimate put worldwide ownership at 774 million in June, up 4.5% from 741 million in December 2025.

A provider can see a large commercial opportunity without expecting an immediate revival in speculative turnover. The addressable holder base and the revenue generated by each active customer are separate questions.

Existing holders moving trading to a bank or brokerage would shift market share, dormant customers returning would increase activity within an existing investor base, and first-time buyers would expand participation.

Three illustrative customer routes show why a brokerage gaining business would not necessarily mean more people own crypto.

An existing holder could move trading from an exchange to a bank or brokerage. The provider gains share without necessarily adding a crypto owner.

A dormant holder could resume trading through the added access. Activity could rise within the existing investor base.

A first-time buyer could enter crypto through a familiar financial platform. This would broaden participation.

Migration shifts share, reactivation restores activity, and first-time adoption expands participation.

This film festival logo proves originality isn't dead in graphic design

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Explore projects, trends and inspiring design work.

Packed with digital art tutorials, inspiration and expert advice from ImagineFX.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

KIFF's redesigned logo feels fresh and alive.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Film festivals don't have a direct impact on most people's lives. Few of us actually get around to attending one, and the films they feature rarely reach our nearest arts cinema, let alone the local multiplex. Yet, whether we ever buy a ticket to one, film festivals really do matter.

This is where new talent, both in front of and behind the camera, gets to show people what they're made of; where fresh and off-the-wall ideas get their first airing in public. A typical festival is a bubbling cauldron of visual styles, storytelling techniques and new voices, which will then gradually filter into mainstream cinema, advertising, music videos and more.

Occasionally too, a festival hit will break out in its own right. Reservoir Dogs started its journey at Sundance; Parasite at Cannes. Even if you've seen neither film, you'll have felt their ripple effects on culture in general.

Wth all that heated experimentation and ambition, you'd think a film festival logo would be another canvas for creativity. But too often, these events seem to just throw together a bunch of elements (a camera, a film reel, a clapperboard) without much thought. So it was a pleasant surprise to see the logo for the Kolkata International Film Festival (KIFF) does something very different.

Kolkata, formerly known as Calcutta, is the capital of West Bengal in India. This year the 32nd edition of its annual film festival runs from 16 to 23 December. Recently, its organisers revealed this new logo (shown above), which is the work of Sandip Ray.

Sandip is not only a filmmaker himself, he's also the son of the legendary Satyajit Ray: a director who was also a designer working on book covers, film posters and typefaces. And in this logo, Sandip has done his father proud.

Where the typical film festival logo juggles several references, this one commits to a single idea, and is all the better for it. Heavy black letters are cut into by burnt-orange triangles, each edged with sprocket holes like a strip of film.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

If someone had described that to me before I'd seen it, I'd probably have thought "That sounds awful". Film strips are the clip art of cinema: as big a cliché as a popcorn box, and increasingly anachronistic to boot.

But here, the execution is everything. Crucially, Sandip hasn't used the film strips as decoration of the logo, but as its foundation. They're not just a way of adding interest to the logo design; they are the logo design. It's the difference between committing to an idea and not actually having one in the first place.

To my mind, the triangles could be seen as doubling up as play and rewind buttons… although admittedly this might just be me.

Overall, the important thing about this logo is that it doesn’t look like anything else. I've spent so many years looking at perfectly formed sans serifs that it's made me weary. And the idea that more and more people are going to use AI tools that serve up an average of this style depresses me even more.

KIFF’s logo, in contrast, is chunky, angular and a bit odd. Some people might dislike it for that very reason. But I love the fact that two colours and one bold idea are all it needs to be instantly recognisable, and that’s got to be an inspiration for logo designers everywhere.

Tom May is an award-winning journalist specialising in art, design, photography and technology. He is the author of the books The 50 Greatest Designers (Arcturus) and Great TED Talks: Creativity (Pavilion). Zelda: Ocarina Of Time "Off To A Great Start", Says Nintendo

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Nintendo has released the Q&A translation from its latest financial briefing, with president Shuntaro Furukawa stating that Zelda: Ocarina of Time is "off to a great start".

The comment comes off the back of a question related to the decrease in hardware sales for the Switch 2, which saw a dip of 37% year-on-year. Furukawa-san explained that Japan saw a significant spike in sales prior to the price revision in May before settling down after. Overseas, meanwhile, Nintendo allowed customers ample time to purchase the Switch 2 before the price went up while also offering multiple bundles.

With the release of the Zelda 40th Anniversary console, Furukawa-san said that this "contributed to strong sales in various regions". At the time of the Q&A, he explains that while it's too early to provide specific figures for Ocarina of Time, the game is off to a great start, with Nintendo in good shape for the coming holiday season.

Nintendo usually holds off on providing specific sales figures until its quarterly financial briefings, so it's possible we might not know how well Ocarina of Time sold for a little while yet. That said, if it's performing particularly well, it's equally likely that Nintendo will provide a separate announcement via its investor relations page and social media accounts. We'll keep an eye out either way.

For the UK at least, we know that the physical release was the second-biggest in series history, beating Breath of the Wild yet falling short of Tears of the Kingdom, with the Switch's high install base likely a big factor for the latter. As for console sales, Nintendo is maintaining its current forecast of 16 million total sales for the fiscal year.

Oh, So That’s Who’s Buying All Those New Subaru Trailseeker EVs…

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

After EV sales in the US crashed into a brick wall last year, the Subaru Trailseeker has been one of the few points of light. Newly introduced this year, Trailseeker has racked up a relatively decent sales track record in the crowded SUV field. As for whether or not Subaru can continue to grow its EV sales in the US, the automaker’s elaborate, multi-faceted “Subaru Loves Pets” campaign has emerged as the secret weapon…

The Subaru Loves Pets campaign first appeared 18 years ago, back in 2008. A lot of demographic and cultural water has passed under the bridge since then. More so than in past generations, pets have taken on the role of family members equal to, if not superior to, anyone else in the household. And if that’s the case, shouldn’t pets have their own seatbelts, too?

Now Subaru’s longtime relationship with the pet-centered population is beginning to muscle into EV territory. One indication of a connection between pet and EV preferences surfaced in October, when Subaru released a new report indicating that pets wield a noticeable influence on purchasing  preferences in the US auto market overall.

Commissioned from the firm Talker Research, the report is based on interviews with 2,000 and pet owners in the US. Of that cohort, a resounding majority of 73% said they are more likely to drive for the holidays, specifically in order to travel with their pet.

“As the holidays approach, more than two-thirds (68%) said their pet will affect their travel plans this year, and nearly half (45%) have turned down travel opportunities that couldn’t accommodate their pet,” Subaru adds.

Households with pets also have distinct priorities for a vehicle. “Plenty of room between pets, passengers, and luggage tops the list of features pet owners say matter when traveling with an animal,” Suburu reports.

“More than one in four (26%) prioritize cargo space for crates, carriers, beds, or gear. Among hybrid and EV drivers, that climbs to 35%,” the automaker further notes.

If you missed the money quote, it’s that thing about 35% of hybrid and EV drivers prioritizing space for pet gear, compared to just 26% of drivers overall. In addition to the traveling-with-pets angle, that generational difference also reflects a sharp generational divergence in attitudes towards EVs.

“Electric vehicles have become a political flashpoint in Washington. But new national polling finds the biggest divide among potential EV buyers isn’t political. It’s generational,” reports the advocacy organization ZETA (the Zero Emission Transportation Association) in September.

“Seventy-five percent of adults ages 18–34 and 72% of adults ages 35–49 say they are likely to purchase or lease a new EV within the next five years,” ZETA elaborates. “Among adults ages 50–64, that number falls to 50%. Among those 65 and older, it drops to just 23%, a striking 52-point gap between the youngest and oldest Americans.”

Now let’s take another look at the Subaru survey. Talker Research found that 75% of Gen Z and 70% of millennials traveled with a pet within the past five years, compared with just 58% of Gen X and only 40% of baby boomers (looking at you, Mitt Romney).

A similar generational difference surfaced in other areas of the survey:

Younger pet owners are also more likely to say they plan travel around their pet, with 42% of Gen Z and millennials doing so compared with 27% of Gen X and boomers.

Among pet owners who haven’t yet road-tripped with their pet, 74% of Gen Z and millennials would consider it, compared with 54% of Gen X and boomers.

Subaru pulled back on its overall electrification plan for the US market after last year’s EV sales crash, but it did follow through on the new Trailseeker, offered for sale on a limited basis in January of this year. Volume deliveries to dealers began in April of this year, and in May the Trailseeker racked up 1,074 in sales. The number is vanishingly small compared to industry leader Tesla but a promising start in the challenging US market. By August, Trailseeker had a total of almost 5,000 in sales for the year to its credit, rising to 6,006 sold by the end of October.

All else being equal, recognition from Wards Auto may help keep the momentum going. On October 13, Subaru announced that the Trailseeker earned a slot in the 2026 Wards 10 Best Engines & Propulsion Systems list.

“For 2026, Wards evaluated 26 powertrains from returning winners as well as all-new or significantly updated cars, trucks, or SUVs. They were evaluated on several criteria, including power, torque, refinement, efficiency, and innovation,” Subaru notes.

“Its 375 horsepower combined with immediate electric powertrain response always satisfies. Its range estimates also hold up well in real-world driving, interstate and all. Yet Subaru keeps the price completely reasonable given such capability,” said Robin Warner, who manages the 10 Best Engines & Propulsion Systems program.

By “completely reasonable,” Warner means the Trailseeker’s starting MSRP of $39,995 and its estimated range of 281 miles. According to Subaru, the Trailseeker can go from 10% to 80% charged in as little as 28 minutes, with up to 150 kW on a DC-fast charger.

“With an onboard battery preconditioning system, enabled either by navigating to a charging destination or by the driver via touchscreen controls, the Trailseeker can charge its battery in cold climates — as low as 14 degrees Fahrenheit — at speeds comparable to those in normal weather conditions,” Subaru also advises.

In addition to the seal of approval from Wards, on October 29 Subaru announced that Trailseeker earned the “Car of the Year” award in the 2027 TopGear.com US Car Awards program

The automaker cites Top Gear US Correspondent Alex Kalogiannis, who was particularly enthused about the vehicle’s off-road performance. ““Off the beaten path, the set-and-forget X-MODE figures out all the tricky terrain stuff as you trundle through the bush, and with about 8.5 inches of ground clearance, it can scale all but the scariest obstacles in your path,” Kalogiannis remarked, somwhat colorfully.

“People still carry a torch for the WRX and its many rally-inspired incarnations, but the heart of Subaru fandom now is its dutiful, rugged, useful wagon – the Trailseeker,” he added.

That remains to be seen. Trailseeker will have to compete for the hearts and minds of Subaru fans against other models in the automaker’s lineup, including gasmobiles. The SUV-styled Outback wagon, for example, clocked 118,470 in sales from January to the end of October this year, and that was not the only Subaru model to hit six figures.

Still, US President Donald Trump has provided Subaru and other automakers with a new opportunity grow their EV sales profile this year. On February 28 the President launched his war against Iran, sending global oil prices on an upwards trajectory and improving the affordability case for EVs regardless of the loss of the $7,500 federal EV tax credit, which Trump and his Republican majority in Congress axed last fall.

Ever since dropping the first bombs on Iran, Trump has repeatedly claimed that the war is over, or almost over, or over any minute. In the latest pronouncement, on  Monday Trump said that multilateral negotiations over a peace settlement in Iran are in a state of “positive momentum toward an end.” And if you believe that…

Photo: Subaru has racked up a fairly respectable EV sales record so far this year for its new Trailseeker SUV (2026 Trailseeker cropped, courtesy of Subaru).

How to dance on a driveable car in Fortnite Treasure Hunters

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Weekly quests will have you attempting to check off all kinds of strange tasks in Fortnite. One of the weirder ones you’ll encounter in Treasure Hunters asks you to dance on a driveable car.

Quests like this one can be tough to complete, as you’re tasked with seeking out a specific item around the vast island. If you’re struggling to get this mission done, here’s how to dance on a driveable car in Fortnite.

A driveable car is any vehicle you can find and hop behind the wheel of around the Fortnite island. In Treasure Hunters, there are a few different types of cars you can find while exploring, including:

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

Drievable cars can be found all around the Fortnite island. Overall, though, they’re most commonly found sitting on roads and parked near main, named POIs. The tough part of finding these vehicles is that none of them are guaranteed to spawn in every round. Instead, they only have a chance of appearing in each match, similar to how the spawning for chests works.

There’s a chance you may encounter a driveable car while exploring around most areas on the island. Some of the overall best spots to check for them, though, are as follows.

To complete the dance on a driveable car quest in Fortnite, you need to locate a vehicle that can be driven, hop on top of it, and perform any dancing emote. As soon as you do, this quest will be complete.

For this mission, you can either simply jump on top of any driveable car, or instead actually enter a Reboot Van, switch to a roof seat, and perform a dancing emote while situated there. Both options work for this quest as long as you’re technically dancing on top of the vehicle.

There’s plenty more to discover and work on around the island after checking off this goal. Next, you might want to learn how to track how many chests you’ve opened, what all the loot odds and drop rates are, and how to get the Sweet Dreams Sprite.
